    6| /// A simple 3D DDA (Digital Differential Analyzer) Raycaster
    7| fn cast_ray(
    8|     grid: &VoxelGrid,
    9|     x: f32,
   10|     y: f32,
   11|     z: f32,
   12|     dx: f32,
   13|     dy: f32,
   14|     dz: f32,
   15|     max_steps: usize,
   16| ) -> Option<(usize, usize, usize, u8)> {
   17|     let step_x = if dx > 0.0 { 1 } else { -1 };
   18|     let step_y = if dy > 0.0 { 1 } else { -1 };
   19|     let step_z = if dz > 0.0 { 1 } else { -1 };
   20| 
   21|     let mut t_max_x = if dx != 0.0 {
   22|         (x.floor() + if dx > 0.0 { 1.0 } else { 0.0 } - x) / dx
   23|     } else {
   24|         f32::INFINITY
   25|     };
   26|     let mut t_max_y = if dy != 0.0 {
   27|         (y.floor() + if dy > 0.0 { 1.0 } else { 0.0 } - y) / dy
   28|     } else {
   29|         f32::INFINITY
   30|     };
   31|     let mut t_max_z = if dz != 0.0 {
   32|         (z.floor() + if dz > 0.0 { 1.0 } else { 0.0 } - z) / dz
   33|     } else {
   34|         f32::INFINITY
   35|     };
   36| 
   37|     let t_delta_x = if dx != 0.0 {
   38|         (1.0 / dx).abs()
   39|     } else {
   40|         f32::INFINITY
   41|     };
   42|     let t_delta_y = if dy != 0.0 {
   43|         (1.0 / dy).abs()
   44|     } else {
   45|         f32::INFINITY
   46|     };
   47|     let t_delta_z = if dz != 0.0 {
   48|         (1.0 / dz).abs()
   49|     } else {
   50|         f32::INFINITY
   51|     };
   52| 
   53|     let mut ix = x as isize;
   54|     let mut iy = y as isize;
   55|     let mut iz = z as isize;
   56| 
   57|     for _ in 0..max_steps {
   58|         if t_max_x < t_max_y {
   59|             if t_max_x < t_max_z {
   60|                 ix += step_x;
   61|                 t_max_x += t_delta_x;
   62|             } else {
   63|                 iz += step_z;
   64|                 t_max_z += t_delta_z;
   65|             }
   66|         } else {
   67|             if t_max_y < t_max_z {
   68|                 iy += step_y;
   69|                 t_max_y += t_delta_y;
   70|             } else {
   71|                 iz += step_z;
   72|                 t_max_z += t_delta_z;
   73|             }
   74|         }
   75| 
   76|         if ix < 0
   77|             || iy < 0
   78|             || iz < 0
   79|             || ix >= grid.width() as isize
   80|             || iy >= grid.height() as isize
   81|             || iz >= grid.depth() as isize
   82|         {
   83|             break;
   84|         }
   85| 
   86|         let vx = ix as usize;
   87|         let vy = iy as usize;
   88|         let vz = iz as usize;
   89| 
   90|         let v = grid.get(vx, vy, vz);
   91|         if v != VOXEL_AIR {
   92|             return Some((vx, vy, vz, v));
   93|         }
   94|     }
   95|     None
   96| }
   97| 
   98| /// Bakes lighting into the VoxelGrid using Monte Carlo Path Tracing
   99| pub fn bake_path_traced_lighting(grid: &mut VoxelGrid) {
  100|     let w = grid.width();
  101|     let h = grid.height();
  102|     let d = grid.depth();
  103| 
  104|     let rays_per_voxel = 64;
  105|     let max_bounces = 1; // 0 for direct only, 1 for one bounce
  106|     let max_steps = 30; // Max ray distance
  107| 
  108|     let mut rng = StdRng::seed_from_u64(42);
  109| 
  110|     // We only need to bake lighting into AIR blocks that are adjacent to solid blocks,
  111|     // but for simplicity we can bake all AIR blocks, or directly bake solid blocks.
  112|     // Actually, baking solid blocks is better. We evaluate light slightly outside the solid block face.
  113|     // Since our map_voxel_grid just uses `grid.get_light(x, y, z)` for the solid block itself,
  114|     // we can just store the light level in the solid block!
  115| 
  116|     for z in 0..d {
  117|         for y in 0..h {
  118|             for x in 0..w {
  119|                 let v = grid.get(x, y, z);
  120|                 if v == VOXEL_AIR || v == VOXEL_LIGHT {
  121|                     continue;
  122|                 }
  123| 
  124|                 // It's a solid block. Let's do path tracing from its center slightly offset by normals.
  125|                 // To keep it simple, we shoot rays from the center of the block. If it immediately hits itself,
  126|                 // we ignore it. Actually, start rays from the adjacent AIR voxels.
  127| 
  128|                 let mut total_light = 0.0;
  129| 
  130|                 // Find an adjacent air voxel to act as the emission point
  131|                 let mut px = x as f32 + 0.5;
  132|                 let mut py = y as f32 + 0.5;
  133|                 let mut pz = z as f32 + 0.5;
  134| 
  135|                 // Shift point slightly towards the air
  136|                 if x > 0 && grid.get(x - 1, y, z) == VOXEL_AIR {
  137|                     px -= 0.55;
  138|                 } else if x < w - 1 && grid.get(x + 1, y, z) == VOXEL_AIR {
  139|                     px += 0.55;
  140|                 } else if y > 0 && grid.get(x, y - 1, z) == VOXEL_AIR {

  141|                     py -= 0.55;
  142|                 } else if y < h - 1 && grid.get(x, y + 1, z) == VOXEL_AIR {
  143|                     py += 0.55;
  144|                 } else if z > 0 && grid.get(x, y, z - 1) == VOXEL_AIR {
  145|                     pz -= 0.55;
  146|                 } else if z < d - 1 && grid.get(x, y, z + 1) == VOXEL_AIR {
  147|                     pz += 0.55;
  148|                 } else {
  149|                     continue; // Completely surrounded by solids, no light needed
  150|                 }
  151| 
  152|                 for _ in 0..rays_per_voxel {
  153|                     // Random spherical direction
  154|                     let theta = rng.random::<f32>() * 2.0 * std::f32::consts::PI;
  155|                     let phi = (rng.random::<f32>() * 2.0 - 1.0).acos();
  156|                     let dx = phi.sin() * theta.cos();
  157|                     let dy = phi.cos();
  158|                     let dz = phi.sin() * theta.sin();
  159| 
  160|                     if let Some((_hx, _hy, _hz, hit_type)) =
  161|                         cast_ray(grid, px, py, pz, dx, dy, dz, max_steps)
  162|                     {
  163|                         if hit_type == VOXEL_LIGHT {
  164|                             total_light += 1.0;
  165|                         } else if max_bounces > 0 {
  166|                             // Secondary bounce (simplified)
  167|                             // We don't do a full recursive call to avoid stack/performance issues,
  168|                             // just a small probability addition to simulate ambient bounce.
  169|                             // If we hit a wall, it bounces a bit of light.
  170|                             total_light += 0.05;
  171|                         }
  172|                     }
  173|                 }
  174| 
  175|                 // Normalize and map to 0-15 scale for compatibility with voxel_mapper
  176|                 let mut light_level = ((total_light / rays_per_voxel as f32) * 15.0 * 2.0) as u8;
  177|                 if light_level > 15 {
  178|                     light_level = 15;
  179|                 }
  180| 
  181|                 grid.set_light(x, y, z, light_level);
  182|             }
  183|         }
  184|     }
  185| }
  186|

   66|                         let mut is_light = false;

   70|                             let fixture2 = lx >= 10 && lx < 12 && lz >= 5 && lz < 11;

   74|                         }

   78|                             voxels.set(vx, vh - 2, vz, VOXEL_AIR); // hole
   79|                             voxels.set(vx, vh - 1, vz, VOXEL_LIGHT); // light emitting block above
   80|                         } else {
   81|                             voxels.set(vx, vh - 2, vz, VOXEL_CEILING);
   82|                         }
   83|                     }
   84|                 }
   85|             }

Level builder: for each grid cell, for each local voxel: floor set, walls set (h loop up to wh), ceiling. Note wall height loop re-writes the same column for each of the 4 wall-side local voxels — each wall column: only set when the local voxel is on the wall side, so each column gets written once per (vx,vz) on the border. Total: O(grid_cells × rs² × wh) = O(V) writes but with `set` doing bounds check + index each. Fine. But note: interior wall corners double-set (north+east both true → h loop runs twice for same column) — minor redundant writes (up to 2x), Low.

Also note the wall is only 1 voxel thick at room borders; the wall between two adjacent rooms is shared: room A's east wall at its last column, room B's west wall at its first column — two separate voxel columns, i.e., walls between rooms are 2 voxels thick. Design.

Now check the BSP `build` line numbers and clip_polygons lines for citation, plus `contains_point` in bsp.rs. Already have. Let me check exact line numbers of the recursion in bsp.rs and the exponential branch. Also the `Solid::subtract`/`union` line numbers (already seen). And check architecture.rs line numbers for validate_architecture nested loops and host() scan, and CeilingPlan::zone_at, CirculationSpine::nearest.

Also grep for where the domain files use `1 << self.depth` overflow: depth up to... SVO::new(depth, world_size) — no bounds on depth; depth >= 64 → `1 << depth` UB (panic in debug, wrap in release). For 21 bits coords, depth should be ≤ 21. `1 << self.depth` with depth=63 → overflow panic. That's a robustness issue — Low.

Also the get_recursive: `if node_idx >= self.nodes.len() { return None; }` — bounds guard. But set_recursive has no such guard — it indexes directly; if the tree has a child_mask bit set but the child node was never allocated... can that happen? Only via set path, which allocates. But there's a subtle corruption: on split, `child_mask = if voxel_type == 0 {0} else {0xFF}`; then set_recursive descends into the new Internal node (indexes child at octant_idx — the child was just pushed so exists). Then it sets child_mask bit (v_type != 0). OK.

But there IS a real bug: when writing air (v_type == 0) into an internal node where the target octant's mask bit is 0 (never set): `child_node_idx = child_base_index + octant_idx` — the child node slot EXISTS (all 8 slots allocated at split time) so no OOB. When writing air into a leaf that is air already with same attributes → early return. When writing air into a leaf with solid: splits, child_mask = 0xFF (because voxel_type != 0)... then recurses into octant with v_type=0; mask bit stays 1 (since `if v_type != 0`); the leaf becomes air. Then collapse_if_uniform: 8 leaves; not all equal (only one is air, others solid) → no collapse. So the tree now has an internal node whose mask says the octant is occupied but the child is an air leaf. `is_empty()` → child_mask == 0? No: 0xFF → false. So is_empty() reports non-empty. And `get()` for that octant returns the leaf's values (0,0,[0;3],0) — same as empty air. Memory retained. If the whole volume is cleared to air one voxel at a time, tree never collapses below internal nodes with mask 0xFF and air leaves → is_empty() stays false, memory stays. So: clear-to-air doesn't reclaim and is_empty lies. That's a genuine correctness bug (Medium-High). Fix: clear mask bit when v_type == 0 and child is uniform air (or after recursion check child is air leaf → clear bit; then collapse_if_uniform handles rest).

Also collapse_if_uniform leaves dead nodes in arena — collapse replaces Internal with Leaf but children remain allocated at the tail (no reclamation). Repeated set/clear → arena grows monotonically; with streaming generation (regenerate chunk per epoch), this is unbounded growth — High/Medium. Fix: free-list of node slots or use a slab with freelist; or accept and periodically compact. Also note SvoNode is Copy so a swap-based free list is easy.

Another subtle issue in `set_recursive` leaf-split: it pushes 8 children BEFORE converting the node; child_base_index = len before push. But `self.nodes[node_idx] = Internal{...}` — no reallocation issue since node_idx is an index, not reference (they index fresh each time — good, they avoided borrow issues by re-indexing; correct).

But wait — there's a genuine alias bug risk: after `self.nodes.push(...)` inside the Leaf branch, `node_idx` remains valid. Then `self.set_recursive(node_idx, ...)` — fine.

One more: `get_recursive` at depth 0 for Internal — returns None — but can an Internal exist at depth 0? set_recursive only creates Internal when depth>0, so no. OK.

Morton: O(1), fine.

- 368-410: with_advanced_gate: clone-heavy, max() scan, retain, and re-sort via with_drifts_and_supplies.
- 424-427 fingerprint.
- 250-285 with_drifts_and_supplies sorting.

  231| impl RealitySnapshot {
  232|     const MAGIC: u32 = 0x5254_5905; // "RTY" v5: v4 plus the delirium tier
  233|     const STAMP_WORDS: usize = 7;
  234|     const DRIFT_WORDS: usize = 5;
  235| 
  236|     pub fn empty() -> Self {
  237|         Self::default()
  238|     }
  239| 
  240|     pub fn new(stamps: Vec<AnomalyStateStamp>) -> Self {
  241|         Self::with_drifts(stamps, Vec::new())
  242|     }
  243| 
  244|     pub fn with_drifts(stamps: Vec<AnomalyStateStamp>, drifts: Vec<FabricDriftStamp>) -> Self {
  245|         Self::with_drifts_and_supplies(stamps, drifts, Vec::new())
  246|     }
  247| 
  248|     pub fn with_drifts_and_supplies(
  249|         stamps: Vec<AnomalyStateStamp>,
  250|         drifts: Vec<FabricDriftStamp>,
  251|         consumed_supplies: Vec<u64>,
  252|     ) -> Self {
  253|         let mut by_id: BTreeMap<AnomalyId, AnomalyStateStamp> = BTreeMap::new();
  254|         for stamp in stamps {
  255|             by_id
  256|                 .entry(stamp.instance_id)
  257|                 .and_modify(|old| {
  258|                     if stamp > *old {
  259|                         *old = stamp;
  260|                     }
  261|                 })
  262|                 .or_insert(stamp);
  263|         }
  264|         let mut by_cell: BTreeMap<(i64, i64), u32> = BTreeMap::new();
  265|         for drift in drifts {
  266|             let epoch = by_cell.entry((drift.cell_x, drift.cell_z)).or_insert(0);
  267|             *epoch = (*epoch).max(drift.epoch);
  268|         }
  269|         let mut consumed = consumed_supplies;
  270|         consumed.sort_unstable();
  271|         consumed.dedup();
  272|         Self {
  273|             stamps: by_id.into_values().collect(),
  274|             drifts: by_cell
  275|                 .into_iter()
  276|                 .filter(|(_, epoch)| *epoch > 0)
  277|                 .map(|((cell_x, cell_z), epoch)| FabricDriftStamp {
  278|                     cell_x,
  279|                     cell_z,
  280|                     epoch,
  281|                 })
  282|                 .collect(),
  283|             consumed_supplies: consumed,
  284|             delirium: 0,
  285|         }
  286|     }
  287| 
  288|     /// The dehydration-driven perception tier (0..=MAX_DELIRIUM).
  289|     pub fn delirium(&self) -> u8 {
  290|         self.delirium
  291|     }
  292| 
  293|     /// The same reality perceived at a different delirium tier.
  294|     pub fn with_delirium(&self, tier: u8) -> Self {
  295|         Self {
  296|             delirium: tier.min(MAX_DELIRIUM),
  297|             ..self.clone()
  298|         }
  299|     }
  300| 
-------- with_advanced_gate
  367|     }
  368| 
  369|     pub fn with_advanced_gate(&self, gate: &TraversalGate, direction: AxisDirection) -> Self {
  370|         let existing = self.lookup(gate.instance_id);
  371|         // Streaming can report the same crossing more than once, so an
  372|         // identical gate+direction event is idempotent.  The opposite
  373|         // direction is a real traversal, however: a Red Room loop advances
  374|         // by walking back and forth through its one authored checkpoint.
  375|         if existing.is_some_and(|stamp| {
  376|             gate.id == stamp.last_gate_id && direction == stamp.travel_direction
  377|         }) {
  378|             return self.clone();
  379|         }
  380| 
  381|         // Epoch is the snapshot-wide encounter revision, not a per-instance
  382|         // counter. That makes the most recently entered recursive Red Room
  383|         // unambiguous even when a child encounter has a different stable id.
  384|         let next_epoch = self
  385|             .stamps
  386|             .iter()
  387|             .map(|stamp| stamp.epoch)
  388|             .max()
  389|             .unwrap_or(0)
  390|             .saturating_add(1);
  391|         let next = transition_for(gate, direction, existing, next_epoch);
  392|         let mut stamps = self.stamps.clone();
  393|         stamps.retain(|stamp| stamp.instance_id != gate.instance_id);
  394|         stamps.push(AnomalyStateStamp::new(
  395|             gate.instance_id,
  396|             next.epoch,
  397|             gate.plane,
  398|             gate.axis,
  399|             direction,
  400|             next.phase,
  401|             next.loop_count,
  402|             gate.id,
  403|         ));
  404|         Self {
  405|             delirium: self.delirium,
  406|             ..Self::with_drifts_and_supplies(
  407|                 stamps,
  408|                 self.drifts.clone(),
  409|                 self.consumed_supplies.clone(),
  410|             )
  411|         }
  412|     }
-------- fingerprint
  419|         }
  420|         mix64(hash)
  421|     }
  422| 
  423|     pub fn fingerprint(&self) -> u64 {
  424|         let words = self.words_without_fingerprint();
  425|         Self::hash_words(&words)
  426|     }
  427| 
  428|     fn words_without_fingerprint(&self) -> Vec<u32> {
  429|         let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(
  430|             4 + self.stamps.len() * Self::STAMP_WORDS
  431|                 + self.drifts.len() * Self::DRIFT_WORDS
  432|                 + self.consumed_supplies.len() * 2,
  433|         );
  434|         out.push(Self::MAGIC);
  435|         out.push(self.stamps.len() as u32);
  436|         for stamp in &self.stamps {
  437|             out.extend_from_slice(&[
  438|                 stamp.instance_id as u32,
  439|                 (stamp.instance_id >> 32) as u32,
  440|                 stamp.epoch,
  441|                 stamp.gate_plane_milli as u32,
  442|                 (stamp.gate_axis as u32)
  443|                     | ((stamp.travel_direction as u32) << 8)
  444|                     | ((stamp.phase as u32) << 16)
  445|                     | ((stamp.loop_count as u32) << 24),
  446|                 stamp.last_gate_id as u32,
  447|                 (stamp.last_gate_id >> 32) as u32,
  448|             ]);
  449|         }
  450|         out.push(self.drifts.len() as u32);
  451|         for drift in &self.drifts {
  452|             out.extend_from_slice(&[
  453|                 drift.cell_x as u32,
  454|                 (drift.cell_x >> 32) as u32,
  455|                 drift.cell_z as u32,
  456|                 (drift.cell_z >> 32) as u32,
  457|                 drift.epoch,
  458|             ]);
  459|         }
  460|         out.push(self.consumed_supplies.len() as u32);
  461|         for &id in &self.consumed_supplies {
  462|             out.push(id as u32);
  463|             out.push((id >> 32) as u32);
  464|         }
  465|         out.push(self.delirium as u32);
  466|         out
  467|     }
  468| 
  469|     pub fn to_words(&self) -> Vec<u32> {
  470|         let mut out = self.words_without_fingerprint();
  471|         let fingerprint = Self::hash_words(&out);
  472|         out.push(fingerprint as u32);
  473|         out.push((fingerprint >> 32) as u32);
  474|         out
  475|     }

    /// The *fabric* column plan at world position (wx, wz): the endless,
    /// unplanned office fill between planned corridors and assemblies.
    ///
    /// This is where the wiki's Peripheral Shift lives: `reality` carries a
    /// drift epoch per 40 u cell, advanced by the engine whenever territory
    /// goes unobserved. The epoch re-salts the *cosmetic and porosity*
    /// decisions — wall dropout, doorway direction/position/width framing,
    /// dead lights — while the ceiling territories, porosity climate,
    /// junction posts, and expanse structure stay fixed, so a returning
    /// wanderer recognizes the neighborhood but never the hallways. Every
    /// decision reads its epoch at the deciding lattice cell's own anchor,
    /// so a wall is rebuilt whole even when a drift-cell boundary crosses it,
    /// and at strain tier 0 the binary-tree doorway rule holds per cell at
    /// any epoch mix — a provisioned wanderer's labyrinth stays globally
    /// connected through every rearrangement. Under strain (`delirium` > 0,
    /// the price of mismanaged provisions) the guarantee erodes on purpose:
    /// guaranteed doorways brick over into dead-end pockets, second
    /// doorways thin, and at deep strain rare 0.8 u slips hide in walls
    /// that offer no doorway at all.
    pub(crate) fn column_plan_in_reality(
        noise: &dyn NoiseProvider,
        seed: u32,
        tuning: &LevelTuning,
        reality: &RealitySnapshot,
        wx: f32,
        wz: f32,
    ) -> ColumnPlan {
        // ---- ceiling field -------------------------------------------------
        let ceiling_band = Self::fabric_ceiling_band(noise, seed, wx, wz);
        let ceiling_units = Self::fabric_ceiling_height(noise, seed, wx, wz, ceiling_band);
        let fx = wx.rem_euclid(FABRIC_CELL);
        let fz = wz.rem_euclid(FABRIC_CELL);
        let boundary_x = (wx / FABRIC_CELL).floor() as i64;
        let boundary_z = (wz / FABRIC_CELL).floor() as i64;
        // Poche: a division's mass says what kind of division it is. The
        // wall a column sits in is the one bounding its own cell, so the
        // thickness comes from that boundary's rank.
        let west_t = super::fabric_ca::wall_thickness(boundary_x);
        let north_t = super::fabric_ca::wall_thickness(boundary_z);
        let (in_w, in_n) = (fx < west_t, fz < north_t);

        // Circulation. A coarse division that chose to be a corridor clears
        // a band centred on its line -- half into each of the two blocks it
        // separates, which is what makes it one continuous route rather than
        // two parallel gaps. Every finer wall inside that band is suppressed:
        // a corridor interrupted by partitions is not a corridor.
        let in_corridor = {
            let epoch_here = 0;
            let near = |offset: f32, index: i64, axis| {
                offset < super::fabric_ca::CORRIDOR_HALF
                    && super::fabric_ca::is_corridor(noise, seed, index, axis, epoch_here)
            };
            near(fx, boundary_x, super::fabric_ca::Axis::West)

Now let me verify a few remaining details:

1. SparseVoxelOctree `new`: depth can be 0 → `1 << 0 = 1`; get with max_coord 1: only (0,0,0) allowed. depth very large → `1 << self.depth` overflow for depth ≥ 31 (u32 shift overflow in debug; in release, shift of u32 by ≥32 is UB masked... actually Rust: `1u32 << 32` panics in debug, and in release it's defined? No — Rust shifts: `1u32 << 32` is a runtime panic in debug; in release it's "masked"? Rust has defined behavior for overflow checks off: it computes `1 << (32 % 32)`? Actually no — Rust follows LLVM: shift amounts >= bit width are UB unless checked... In Rust, `<<` on integers with shift >= bits is a panic in debug builds; in release builds with overflow-checks off it's still NOT UB because Rust defines shift as `x << (shift % bits)`? Let me recall: Rust documentation: "Shifts are defined for all shift amounts" — the compiler will mask the shift amount to be less than the bit width (e.g. `1u32 << 32 == 1`). Yes, Rust defines this: rhs is masked modulo bit width. So `1u32 << 32 == 1` silently. So `1 << self.depth` for depth=32 → 1 (wrap) — silently wrong behavior: set/get would address only origin. A depth of 21+ is all that fits coordinates. depth > 21 isn't validated. Low severity robustness finding.

2. get_recursive: for octants masked off it returns `Some((0,0,[0;3],0))` — note this conflates "no data" with "air" — fine semantically (air), but the caller can't distinguish — minor.

3. The real SVO bug: set(v_type=0) never clears mask bit (line 202-204), so:
   - `is_empty()` (line ~39) returns false for a fully-cleared tree (all leaves air, mask 0xFF at root).
   - Memory never reclaimed: `collapse_if_uniform` only collapses when all 8 children are identical leaves — if a node's children are air leaves (uniform), it WILL collapse to Leaf(0). Wait — collapse_if_uniform at the parent: after setting one child to air, the 8 children of the parent are not uniform (1 air + 7 solid) → no collapse. But as you clear more voxels, eventually when all 8 children are air leaves, collapse kicks in: node becomes Leaf air. Then the grandparent: 8 children — one is now a Leaf air, others still internals → not uniform... when all 8 become Leaf air → collapse again... So clearing all voxels does collapse the whole tree to root leaf air IF the mask bit clearing happened... wait, but the mask bits: when a child octant is an air leaf and mask bit set... collapse checks children VALUES, not mask bits. So collapse works bottom-up as long as leaves are uniform air. After full clear, tree collapses to a single air Leaf at root, is_empty() → true. Hmm, so is_empty() is eventually correct after full collapse.

   BUT: consider a single voxel set then cleared: root split (8 children: 7 solid? No — wait, root was a Leaf with voxel_type 0 (air, from new()). set(x,y,z,wall): root leaf air + depth>0 → split: child_mask = 0 (voxel_type == 0), 8 air children; root → Internal; recurse: sets octant bit (v_type != 0), descend to child leaf (air): depth>0 → split it: 8 children, mask 0xFF (voxel_type==0? no wait the child leaf has voxel_type=0, so child_mask=0, 8 air children; then recurse, set bit... At each level, the leaf being descended into is air (voxel_type 0) → splits with mask 0; sets its octant bit. Finally at depth 0, leaf becomes wall. So the tree path: each internal node has exactly 1 bit set; collapse never triggers (7 air leaves + 1 internal). 

   Now clear: set(x,y,z,air): root internal; bit stays set (v_type==0 → no change); descend; at depth 1: the child internal; descend to depth 0 leaf; leaf becomes air (depth 0). Collapse at parent: children = 7 air leaves + 1 air leaf = all uniform air → collapse to air leaf! Then up one level: root's children = 7 air leaves + 1 air leaf (collapsed) → uniform → collapse to air leaf. So the tree fully collapses and is_empty() becomes true, and nodes stay in the arena (dead). So the mask-bit bug is mostly benign for is_empty after full collapse, but:

   - While the tree is NOT fully collapsed, `get()` for an octant whose mask bit is set but child is air leaf returns `Some((0,0,[0;3],0))` — identical to the masked-off case; so the mask is effectively a "has data ever been written here" bit, not "is non-empty". The doc says "bit i represents if child i is non-empty (1) or empty air (0)" — the invariant is violated: bits stay set for air children. Consequences:
     a) Memory: an internal node with mask 0xFF whose 8 children are air leaves is never collapsed? No — collapse checks values, so it will collapse. Hmm.
     b) The real consequence: **a voxel that was set then cleared retains its full subtree depth of dead internal nodes until the entire parent subtree becomes uniform**. E.g., set a 2x2x2 block at depth 1 in a large tree: root has 1 bit; the child internal has 8 bits; clear all 8 voxels: child collapses to air leaf, root bit still set with air leaf child; root won't collapse until all its other children are uniform air. The mask bit set with an air-leaf child is then permanent until the whole root subtree collapses. During that time, get() on that octant descends 1 extra level — trivial.
     c) **compress_svdag / canonicalize and serializers** treat mask bits as occupancy — a set bit pointing at an air leaf produces a non-empty subtree in the SVDAG with an all-air leaf, wasting nodes and potentially emitting phantom surfaces. Let me check how compress_svdag and octree_gpu_serializer use child_mask. That would make this finding more severe (cross-module impact). Let me look.

4. Another SVO issue: `collapse_if_uniform` runs on EVERY set that touches an internal node (line 225) — O(8) per level — fine.

5. `set_recursive` early-return when the leaf already has identical attributes (line 156) — BUT this check happens before the split decision at every level — at a Leaf node with depth>0, if attributes equal → return without descending — that's correct.

   One more: `set_recursive` at internal node with `depth` — `let bit = depth - 1;` — if depth == 0 and node is Internal — can't happen.

   Also mask bit: `child_mask |= 1 << octant_idx` when v_type != 0 — if the child subtree later collapses to air... wait we covered it.

6. Also `SparseVoxelOctree::set` bounds check `x >= max_coord` — coordinates must be < 2^depth — fine.

Let me quickly check compress_svdag and octree_gpu_serializer to see how they consume child_mask and whether the stale-bit issue causes real damage. These are outside my assigned scope, but reading them to assess severity is reasonable (I won't audit them fully, just the interaction).
//! Sparse Voxel DAG compression: bottom-up merging of identical subtrees.
//!
//! The SVO arena stores every internal node's eight children as one
//! contiguous block, so the unit of sharing is that block: two internal
//! nodes whose child blocks are structurally identical can point at a single
//! canonical copy. Repetitive architecture (identical ceiling tiles, wall
//! runs, column bays) then costs one subtree instead of hundreds.
//!
//! The result is still a [`SparseVoxelOctree`]: traversal only ever follows
//! `child_base_index`, so lookups and the GPU serializer are agnostic to
//! whether the arena is a tree or a DAG. The one consumer that must NOT
//! receive a DAG is any walk that visits every arena node expecting each to
//! appear once per world position (e.g. collision extraction) — compress
//! after those walks, immediately before upload.

use std::collections::HashMap;

use crate::domain::entities::sparse_voxel_octree::{SparseVoxelOctree, SvoNode};

/// Arena sizes before and after deduplication, for telemetry.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SvdagStats {
    pub nodes_before: usize,
    pub nodes_after: usize,
}

/// A node in canonical form: internals are identified by the id of their
/// interned child block rather than an arena offset, which is what makes
/// structural equality (and therefore hashing) meaningful.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
enum CanonNode {
    Leaf {
        voxel_type: u8,
        color: u32,
        light_rgb: [u8; 3],
        face_occlusion: u8,
    },
    Internal {
        child_base_index: u32,
        child_mask: u8,
    },
}

impl CanonNode {
    fn to_svo(self) -> SvoNode {
        match self {
            CanonNode::Leaf {
                voxel_type,
                color,
                light_rgb,
                face_occlusion,
            } => SvoNode::Leaf {
                voxel_type,
                color,
                light_rgb,
                face_occlusion,
            },
            CanonNode::Internal {
                child_base_index,
                child_mask,
            } => SvoNode::Internal {
                child_base_index,
                child_mask,
            },
        }
    }
}

struct BlockInterner {
    /// Canonical child block -> its base index in the output arena.
    blocks: HashMap<[CanonNode; 8], u32>,
    nodes: Vec<SvoNode>,
}

impl BlockInterner {
    /// Returns the output-arena base of this block, materializing it only
    /// the first time the block's structure is seen.
    fn intern(&mut self, block: [CanonNode; 8]) -> u32 {
        if let Some(&base) = self.blocks.get(&block) {
            return base;
        }
        let base = self.nodes.len() as u32;
        self.nodes.extend(block.iter().map(|canon| canon.to_svo()));
        self.blocks.insert(block, base);
        base
    }

    /// Rebuilds the subtree rooted at `index` bottom-up, sharing every child
    /// block already interned.
    fn canonicalize(&mut self, source: &SparseVoxelOctree, index: usize) -> CanonNode {
        match source.nodes[index] {
            SvoNode::Leaf {
                voxel_type,
                color,
                light_rgb,
                face_occlusion,
            } => CanonNode::Leaf {
                voxel_type,
                color,
                light_rgb,
                face_occlusion,
            },
            SvoNode::Internal {
                child_base_index,
                child_mask,
            } => {
                let mut block = [CanonNode::Leaf {
                    voxel_type: 0,
                    color: 0,
                    light_rgb: [0; 3],
                    face_occlusion: 0,
                }; 8];
                for (octant, slot) in block.iter_mut().enumerate() {
                    *slot = self.canonicalize(source, child_base_index as usize + octant);
                }
                CanonNode::Internal {
                    child_base_index: self.intern(block),
                    child_mask,
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Deduplicates identical subtrees, returning a lookup-equivalent octree
/// whose arena shares one copy of every repeated child block.
pub fn compress_svdag(source: &SparseVoxelOctree) -> (SparseVoxelOctree, SvdagStats) {
    let mut interner = BlockInterner {
        blocks: HashMap::new(),
        nodes: Vec::new(),
    };
    let root_canon = interner.canonicalize(source, source.root);

    // Root last, matching the tree builder's arena convention.
    let root = interner.nodes.len();
    interner.nodes.push(root_canon.to_svo());

    let stats = SvdagStats {
        nodes_before: source.nodes.len(),
        nodes_after: interner.nodes.len(),
    };
    (
        SparseVoxelOctree {
            root,
            nodes: interner.nodes,
            depth: source.depth,
            world_size: source.world_size,
        },
        stats,
    )
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::adapters::material_palette::DEFAULT_MATERIAL_PALETTE;
    use crate::domain::entities::voxel_grid::{VOXEL_CEILING, VOXEL_FLOOR, VOXEL_WALL, VoxelGrid};
    use crate::use_cases::build_octree::BuildOctreeUseCase;

    /// A floor slab plus a periodic wall/ceiling module: the same subtree


compress_svdag canonicalizes ALL 8 children regardless of child_mask — it ignores the mask for recursion (recurses into all 8 slots). Since the SVO always allocates all 8 slots at split, that's safe but wasteful (a node with mask bit for only 1 octant still canonicalizes 8 subtrees). Note: because set() never clears mask bits, SVDAG canonicalization considers the air-leaf children with mask bits set — blocks with stale bits fail to merge with identical blocks that have clean masks? The mask is part of CanonNode::Internal, so two structurally identical subtrees with different stale mask bits won't merge — minor compression loss. Also `child_mask` for leaf-air with set bit: the serializer may emit a child pointer for an octant whose child is an air leaf — but the GPU serializer writes child pointers per mask bit probably; an air leaf node at the next level. Let me peek at octree_gpu_serializer to see how mask is used — a set bit pointing at an all-zero leaf creates a non-empty node with no surfaces... Actually voxel_type 0 leaves produce no surfaces, so visually fine; cost is extra nodes.

Key damage of the stale-mask bug:
1. is_empty() false while stale bits remain (only until full collapse — collapse ignores mask, so eventually correct).
2. SVDAG: canonicalization recurses into all 8 slots regardless (so no mask-driven shortcuts), but the mask bits included in CanonNode::Internal → dedup misses → compression loss. Actually bigger: since set() only ever sets bits (never clears), every node on a path where a voxel was cleared retains a mask that doesn't reflect occupancy; compress then cannot merge e.g. "fully air" subtrees with clean mask 0 vs stale mask 0xFF — but wait: a fully-air subtree would have been collapsed by collapse_if_uniform (all 8 children air leaves → parent becomes Leaf air), so fully-air internal nodes shouldn't exist... except when the 8 children are not uniform — e.g., 1 solid leaf + 7 air leaves, then solid cleared → 8 air leaves → collapse. So fully-air internals collapse. Stale bits persist only on internal nodes whose children are NOT all air (some solid remains elsewhere in the same node's subtree). For those, the stale bit marks an octant whose child is an air LEAF but the node has other solid children → not uniform → no collapse → stale bit persists. Effect: mask claims occupancy where there is none; canonicalize dedup still works (mask is part of key, so identical trees with identical stale bits merge). The concrete user-visible harm is limited: is_empty() can be wrong for partially-cleared trees, and any consumer using child_mask to skip traversal (GPU traversal? octree_gpu_serializer emits child pointers based on mask?) may dereference an air leaf — harmless if it exists (it does). 

Actually wait, there's one more important case: `set(v_type=0)` descending into an octant whose mask bit is CLEAR: child exists (allocated at split). Fine.

What about `set` on a node that was collapsed: root leaf air with depth>0 → split again with mask 0, set bit, etc. Fine.

So the mask bug is Medium severity: violates documented invariant, causes is_empty() false positives, memory/compression inefficiency, potential phantom child pointers in serializers that read mask as occupancy. Not Critical (no crash/race — values still read as air).

Hmm, wait — one more: is there a case where the mask bit is set but the CHILD IS AN INTERNAL whose subtree is fully air? An internal with all-air children would have collapsed. But collapse happens only along the path of the set() call (line 225 only for the node just descended into, recursively up). Yes — collapse_if_uniform is called after recursion at each level on the path, so every ancestor on the path gets a chance. But what about nodes NOT on the path? A stale bit in an ancestor: when we clear the last solid voxel in a sibling subtree, the ancestor's collapse check happens. So all ancestors get collapse checks when their subtree changes. Collapse propagates only if the direct children are all identical leaves. Consider: root has 2 octants with internals (stale bits elsewhere). Clear all voxels in octant A's subtree: A collapses to air leaf. Root's children: leaf-air, internal B (with solid), 6 air leaves → not uniform (B is internal) → no collapse. Fine. Now clear B's subtree: B collapses to air leaf → root's children all air leaves → collapse. So the tree always eventually collapses correctly. The stale-bit bug's net effects: (1) transient is_empty false positives (until collapse), (2) extra get() descent levels — trivial, (3) SVDAG dedup key includes stale masks — dedup unaffected between identical trees; (4) serializers that emit per-mask child slots emit pointers to air leaves — let me verify octree_gpu_serializer quickly.
use crate::domain::entities::sparse_voxel_octree::{SparseVoxelOctree, SvoNode};

/// Represents row-padded SVO data ready for renderer storage upload.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct OctreeGpuData {
    pub texture_width: u32,
    pub texture_height: u32,
    pub texel_data: Vec<u32>,
}

/// GPU adapter that flattens the sparse voxel octree into four words per node.
///
/// DOCUMENTED ENCODING SCHEME (four `u32` lanes, historically RGBA32UI):
/// Each SVO node occupies exactly four 32-bit unsigned integers: R, G, B, A.
///
/// CHANNEL LAYOUT:
/// - **R (x):** Node Type Flag
///   * `0` = Internal Parent Node
///   * `1` = Leaf Voxel Node
///
/// - **G (y):** Payload 1
///   * If Internal Node (`R == 0`): `child_base_index` (uint32) - Index in the texture
///     of the first child. The remaining 7 children are stored contiguously at indices
///     `child_base_index + 1` through `child_base_index + 7`.
///   * If Leaf Node (`R == 1`): `voxel_type` (uint32) - Voxel type ID (e.g., WALL, FLOOR, etc.).
///
/// - **B (z):** Payload 2
///   * If Internal Node (`R == 0`): `child_mask` (uint32) - 8-bit active children mask.
///     Bit `i` is `1` if child `i` is non-empty, and `0` if child `i` is empty air.
///   * If Leaf Node (`R == 1`): `color` (uint32) - 24-bit packed RGB color (`0xRRGGBB`).
///
/// - **A (w):** Payload 3
///   * If Internal Node (`R == 0`): `0` (Padding).
///   * If Leaf Node (`R == 1`): packed lighting:
///     - bits 0-7:   scalar light level, the max of the RGB channels (0-15)
///     - bits 8-13:  neighbor-occupancy mask (+X,-X,+Y,-Y,+Z,-Z)
///       A clear bit is an exposed physical face. Bits 14-15 are reserved.
///     - bits 16-19: red light channel (0-15)
///     - bits 20-23: green light channel (0-15)
///     - bits 24-27: blue light channel (0-15)
pub struct OctreeGpuSerializer;

impl OctreeGpuSerializer {
    /// Serializes SVO nodes for upload, padding the last 1024-node row so
    /// incremental atlas updates retain stable row alignment.
    pub fn serialize_to_gpu_data(octree: &SparseVoxelOctree) -> OctreeGpuData {
        let num_nodes = octree.nodes.len();
        let texture_width = 1024; // Stable node-row width for partial uploads.
        let total_texels = ((num_nodes + texture_width - 1) / texture_width) * texture_width;

        let mut texel_data = Vec::with_capacity(total_texels * 4);

        for node in &octree.nodes {
            match *node {
                SvoNode::Leaf {
                    voxel_type,
                    color,
                    light_rgb,
                    face_occlusion,
                } => {
                    let [r, g, b] = light_rgb;
                    let scalar = r.max(g).max(b) as u32;
                    texel_data.push(1); // R
                    texel_data.push(voxel_type as u32); // G
                    texel_data.push(color); // B
                    texel_data.push(
                        scalar
                            | (face_occlusion as u32) << 8
                            | (r as u32 & 0xF) << 16
                            | (g as u32 & 0xF) << 20
                            | (b as u32 & 0xF) << 24,
                    ); // A
                }
                SvoNode::Internal {
                    child_base_index,
                    child_mask,
                } => {
                    texel_data.push(0); // R
                    texel_data.push(child_base_index); // G
                    texel_data.push(child_mask as u32); // B
                    texel_data.push(0); // A
                }
            }
        }

        // Pad the final row with canonical air so slot updates stay row-aligned.
        let padded_nodes_count = total_texels - num_nodes;
        for _ in 0..padded_nodes_count {
            texel_data.push(1); // Leaf
            texel_data.push(0); // Air
            texel_data.push(0); // Color
            texel_data.push(0); // Light Level
        }

        let texture_height = (total_texels / texture_width) as u32;

        OctreeGpuData {
            texture_width: texture_width as u32,
            texture_height,
            texel_data,
        }
    }
}

/// Converts a dense voxel grid into a collapsed sparse octree.
///
/// Colors are injected because the octree is also a renderer upload artifact;
/// generation itself does not own a display palette.
pub struct BuildOctreeUseCase<'a> {
    palette: &'a dyn MaterialPalette,
}

impl<'a> BuildOctreeUseCase<'a> {
    pub fn new(palette: &'a dyn MaterialPalette) -> Self {
        Self { palette }
    }

    /// Converts a dense VoxelGrid into an SVO of the specified depth.
    pub fn execute(&self, grid: &VoxelGrid, depth: u32, world_size: f32) -> SparseVoxelOctree {
        self.execute_internal::<true>(grid, depth, world_size)
    }

    /// Shared implementation kept generic over the pruning policy so tests can
    /// compare the optimized traversal with the former exhaustive traversal.
    fn execute_internal<const PRUNE_OUTSIDE: bool>(
        &self,
        grid: &VoxelGrid,
        depth: u32,
        world_size: f32,
    ) -> SparseVoxelOctree {
        let mut nodes = Vec::new();

        let root_node =
            self.build_recursive::<PRUNE_OUTSIDE>(grid, &mut nodes, OctreeRegion::root(depth));

        // Push the root node to the end of the nodes array
        let root_idx = nodes.len();
        nodes.push(root_node);

        SparseVoxelOctree {
            root: root_idx,
            nodes,
            depth,
            world_size,
        }
    }

    fn build_recursive<const PRUNE_OUTSIDE: bool>(
        &self,
        grid: &VoxelGrid,
        nodes: &mut Vec<SvoNode>,
        region: OctreeRegion,
    ) -> SvoNode {
        // Every coordinate sampled outside the dense grid is canonical air.
        // Recursive cubes only extend in the positive direction, so when the
        // cube's minimum lies past any grid dimension the former exhaustive
        // traversal could only collapse back to this same leaf. It also could
        // not append arena nodes, which makes this short-circuit byte-for-byte
        // equivalent while avoiding all descendant calls.
        if PRUNE_OUTSIDE && self.region_is_outside_grid(grid, region.origin) {
            return AIR_LEAF;
        }

        if region.depth == 0 {
            let [x, y, z] = region.origin;
            let (v_type, color, ll, fo) = self.get_voxel_attrs(grid, x, y, z);
            return SvoNode::Leaf {
                voxel_type: v_type,
                color,
                light_rgb: ll,
                face_occlusion: fo,
            };
        }


Interesting — build_octree builds bottom-up recursively (good, O(V)) rather than via set(). So the SVO set() API is used by brick_pool test builders and octree_gpu_serializer tests; the production build path is build_octree / build_octree_direct. Let me check build_octree_direct since it likely uses set() (the direct path).
//! Direct-to-SVO emission: build the octree straight from a sampling
//! function, with no dense intermediate 3D array.
//!
//! The classic pipeline allocates a dense grid (megabytes per chunk), fills
//! it, then compresses to an SVO. On low-memory targets that intermediate is
//! pure waste when the source can already answer "what is at (x, y, z)?" —
//! and better, "is this whole cube uniform?". A sampler that can prove
//! uniformity for a region lets the builder emit one leaf and never descend,
//! so cost tracks the *surface* of the geometry, not its volume.
//!
//! The arena layout (children contiguous per block, root pushed last,
//! uniform blocks collapsed to a leaf) matches [`BuildOctreeUseCase`]
//! byte-for-byte, so the two paths are interchangeable and comparable with
//! plain equality in tests.

use crate::domain::entities::sparse_voxel_octree::{SparseVoxelOctree, SvoNode};
use crate::domain::entities::voxel_grid::{VOXEL_AIR, VoxelGrid};
use crate::use_cases::ports::MaterialPalette;

/// The attributes of one voxel as the octree stores them.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SvoLeaf {
    pub voxel_type: u8,
    pub color: u32,
    pub light_rgb: [u8; 3],
    pub face_occlusion: u8,
}

impl SvoLeaf {
    pub const AIR: Self = Self {
        voxel_type: VOXEL_AIR,
        color: 0,
        light_rgb: [0; 3],
        face_occlusion: 0,
    };

    fn to_node(self) -> SvoNode {
        SvoNode::Leaf {
            voxel_type: self.voxel_type,
            color: self.color,
            light_rgb: self.light_rgb,
            face_occlusion: self.face_occlusion,
        }
    }
}

/// A volume the builder can sample point-wise, plus an optional proof that a
/// whole cube is uniform (the pruning that makes direct emission cheap).
pub trait VoxelSampler {
    fn sample(&self, x: u32, y: u32, z: u32) -> SvoLeaf;

    /// `Some(leaf)` when every voxel in the cube at `origin` with edge
    /// `size` provably has these attributes. Returning `None` just means
    /// "descend"; it must never be wrong, only conservative.
    fn uniform_hint(&self, _origin: [u32; 3], _size: u32) -> Option<SvoLeaf> {
        None
    }
}

/// Adapts a dense grid (plus presentation palette) to the sampler port.
/// Out-of-grid coordinates are canonical air, and any cube lying entirely
/// past the grid proves itself uniform — the same pruning the dense builder
/// applies.
pub struct GridSampler<'a> {
    pub grid: &'a VoxelGrid,
    pub palette: &'a dyn MaterialPalette,
}

impl VoxelSampler for GridSampler<'_> {
    fn sample(&self, x: u32, y: u32, z: u32) -> SvoLeaf {
        let (x, y, z) = (x as usize, y as usize, z as usize);
        if x >= self.grid.width() || y >= self.grid.height() || z >= self.grid.depth() {
            return SvoLeaf::AIR;
        }
        let voxel_type = self.grid.get(x, y, z);
        if voxel_type == VOXEL_AIR {
            return SvoLeaf::AIR;
        }
        SvoLeaf {
            voxel_type,
            color: self.palette.color(voxel_type),
            light_rgb: self.grid.get_light_rgb(x, y, z),
            face_occlusion: self.grid.get_face_occlusion(x, y, z),
        }
    }

    fn uniform_hint(&self, origin: [u32; 3], _size: u32) -> Option<SvoLeaf> {
        let outside = origin[0] as usize >= self.grid.width()
            || origin[1] as usize >= self.grid.height()
            || origin[2] as usize >= self.grid.depth();
        outside.then_some(SvoLeaf::AIR)
    }
}

/// Builds an SVO of `depth` levels by recursive sampling, emitting leaves
/// for provably uniform cubes without descending into them.
pub fn build_octree_direct(
    sampler: &dyn VoxelSampler,
    depth: u32,
    world_size: f32,
) -> SparseVoxelOctree {
    let mut nodes = Vec::new();
    let root_node = emit_cube(sampler, &mut nodes, [0; 3], 1 << depth, depth);
    let root = nodes.len();
    nodes.push(root_node);
    SparseVoxelOctree {
        root,
        nodes,
        depth,
        world_size,
    }
}

fn emit_cube(
    sampler: &dyn VoxelSampler,
    nodes: &mut Vec<SvoNode>,
    origin: [u32; 3],
    size: u32,
    depth: u32,
) -> SvoNode {
    if let Some(leaf) = sampler.uniform_hint(origin, size) {
        return leaf.to_node();
    }
    if depth == 0 {
        return sampler.sample(origin[0], origin[1], origin[2]).to_node();
    }

    let half = size / 2;
    let mut children = [SvoLeaf::AIR.to_node(); 8];
    for (octant, child) in children.iter_mut().enumerate() {
        let octant = octant as u32;
        let child_origin = [
            origin[0] + (octant & 1) * half,
            origin[1] + ((octant >> 1) & 1) * half,
            origin[2] + ((octant >> 2) & 1) * half,
        ];
        *child = emit_cube(sampler, nodes, child_origin, half, depth - 1);
    }

    if let Some(leaf) = uniform_leaf(&children) {

- line 68: `frontier.swap_remove(idx)` — O(1) but the *picked* cell is removed only when isolated; the same cell can be re-picked repeatedly while it still has unvisited neighbors... Each iteration either removes a cell or adds one; adds ≤ N, removes ≤ N → O(N) iterations. Each iteration O(1) + small Vec alloc. Good.

Now the probe grid downsampling: `grid.get_light_rgb(vx, vy, vz)` per air voxel — O(1). Overall O(V). Note the halo claim "1-probe halo duplication at grid boundaries" — the code does NOT duplicate any halo; it only samples within the grid. The doc claims halo duplication to avoid seams — the implementation doesn't do it (each probe region is strictly within the voxel grid; no boundary duplication happens). Minor doc-vs-code mismatch. And "Trilinearly interpolates" doc vs nearest sampling. Both doc mismatches — Low.

Also `sample_normalized` uses `.round()` — nearest; seams between chunks if probe grids are per-chunk with no halo — the boundary probes average only interior voxels — the neighboring chunk's edge probe may differ → seam. Doc claims halo duplication prevents this but code has none. Medium? It's a quality claim; the code path is used by web_renderer per chunk (line 57). I'll rate Low-Medium.

Let me also verify: `ProbeGrid::from_voxel_grid` — `(z_start..z_end)` etc. — regions computed with `.max(z_start + 1)` — each probe region is ≥1 voxel. But regions can OVERLAP? z_start = (pz*vox_d)/target_d; z_end = ((pz+1)*vox_d)/target_d — monotonic non-overlapping except when a region is degenerate (z_end = z_start → forced +1 → overlapping next region by 1?). e.g. vox_d=8, target_d=16: pz=0: z 0..1 (0.5→0..1); pz=1: z_start=0.5→0; z_end=1 → max(0+1)=1 → region 0..1; pz=2: 1..2 ... pz=0 and pz=1 both cover voxel row 0 — overlapping samples. When target > source dims, adjacent probes sample the same voxels (duplicated work but consistent, low impact). Not a perf issue since target dims are small.

Now, the "unbounded growth" items:
- SparseVoxelOctree arena: nodes Vec grows on every split; collapse leaves dead nodes; if set() is used for editing (authoring.rs EditOp::Cut uses VoxelGrid not octree; brick_pool tests use octree.set) — the arena growth is bounded by number of set calls × depth in the worst case: each split adds 8 nodes; collapses don't free. A builder that sets every voxel in a 2^d grid creates ~8·2^(3d-3) nodes... same order as the tree itself (each internal node has 8 children slots; arena size = 8 per internal node; for full tree: (8^(d+1)-1)/7·... it's O(leaves·8/7) — the arena is ~8/7× leaves — fine for build; the leak is from churn (set→clear→set cycles on the same volume: each cycle adds 8·depth nodes). Authoring tools could cycle many times → unbounded. Medium.

- RealitySnapshot: stamps grow by instance; drifts grow per drifted 40u cell visited (bounded by play area but unbounded in infinite world); consumed_supplies grows per pickup — unbounded over a long session. Transport (to_words/fingerprint) cost grows linearly with playtime. Low/Medium; identity semantics make eviction hard.

Now the concurrency inventory for the domain layer:
- No shared mutable state, no atomics, no thread_local, no static mut, no unsafe (grep confirmed). All types are plain data. RNG: path_tracer creates its own StdRng (not shared); generate_maze takes &mut R (caller-owned; potential sharing hazard is in the caller — out of scope but note). Determinism: pillar_shape/pit_hazards use mix64 — deterministic, no global. So the concurrency audit for this area: "nothing shared; domain is thread-safe by construction; watch out for StdRng non-determinism if StdRng::from_os_rng or thread_rng used elsewhere" — the only RNG use is seeded StdRng (deterministic) and the trait param. Also note: if generate_maze were called from multiple worker threads with the same rng — not possible with &mut. Good.

One caveat: `Grid`/`Cell` etc. are plain data, `SparseVoxelOctree` — plain. `BTreeMap` local. Nothing to report except a positive note + the note that bake_path_traced_lighting is single-threaded O(V·R·S) and embarrassingly parallel (could be rayon-ized but rayon is dev-only; on wasm there's no threads — vectorization note).

Also determinism: path_tracer seeds 42 → deterministic bake; but note the RNG sequence depends on the ORDER of voxels iterated (z,y,x loops) — deterministic. Fine.

Findings list (severity/file:line/issue/fix):

1. **Medium** (correctness-adjacent) sparse_voxel_octree.rs:202-204: set() with voxel_type=0 never clears the child_mask bit → mask no longer means "non-empty"; is_empty() (line 39-45) can report false for effectively-empty trees, serializers emit child pointers to air leaves, SVDAG dedup keys carry stale bits. Fix: after the recursive set, if child is an air leaf (or collapse produced one), clear the bit; or recompute child_mask from child states after recursion.

2. **Medium** sparse_voxel_octree.rs:170-225 + 230-266: collapse leaves dead child nodes in the arena; repeated set/clear cycles grow nodes Vec without bound (no free list, no compaction). Fix: freelist (SvoNode is Copy → easy swap-remove reuse) or arena compaction before upload; at minimum document and add a `compact()`.

3. **Low** sparse_voxel_octree.rs:59-64: get() returns Some((0,...)) for mask-cleared octants, conflating "empty octant" with "voxel data"; callers can't distinguish. Also get_recursive indexes child slot for masked-off octants? No — it returns before indexing. Fine. The conflation is intentional-ish; note it.

4. **Low** sparse_voxel_octree.rs:120-121 (and get): `1 << self.depth` — depth ≥ 32 wraps (u32 shift is masked in Rust) → set/get silently address only origin; no validation of depth ≤ 21. Fix: assert depth ≤ 21 (MORTON_MAX_COORD) or clamp.

5. **High** path_tracer.rs:116-184: bake is O(V_solid·R·S) = ~1920 inner steps per solid voxel; per chunk (e.g., 32³=32768 with ~50% solid → ~31M steps) and repeated per chunk; plus per-voxel redundant ray casts from nearly identical origins (adjacent solid voxels share faces — 6× redundant hemisphere integrals). Also transcendental per ray (acos/sin/cos) ~3 per ray. Fix: (a) compute one irradiance estimate per air voxel (e.g., single DDA visibility from each air voxel to each light, or a light-propagation/flood fill) and scatter to adjacent solids; (b) replace cos/sin direction sampling with a precomputed low-discrepancy direction table; (c) early-out: cast only toward known light voxels (collect light list first, O(L) small).

6. **Low** path_tracer.rs:106: max_steps=30 hard cap — rays that would travel >30 voxels return None and contribute 0 → distant lights invisible; inconsistent with room scales (rooms 16+ voxels, corridors long). Fix: derive max_steps from grid diagonal, or make it a parameter.

7. **Low** path_tracer.rs:152-172: ~half the rays start 0.05 inside the adjacent air voxel but point back at the solid → immediate self-hit adds constant 0.05 "bounce" bias to nearly every voxel (plus 0.025 average), flattening light contrast. Fix: cosine-weighted hemisphere sampling oriented by the face normal instead of full sphere.

8. **Medium** path_tracer.rs:108: single StdRng seeded 42 — deterministic (good) but every chunk bake consumes the identical RNG stream → identical noise pattern per chunk if called per chunk (repeatable artifacts); also single-threaded: bake is embarrassingly parallel — rayon exists only in dev-deps; on wasm no threads, but chunk-level parallelism possible via worker (out of scope). Also if bake is called for chunk N+1 the sequence continues — determinism per chunk depends on call order... it's seeded fresh at 42 each call, so per-chunk identical noise → banding/periodicity artifacts. Fix: seed with (level, chunk coords) hash for spatial variation.

9. **Medium** probe_grid.rs:163-186: `sample_normalized` doc says trilinear but implements nearest (round); also doc claims halo duplication (from_voxel_grid) but no halo is emitted → boundary probes only average interior voxels; adjacent chunk probe volumes can disagree at seams. Fix: implement actual trilinear or fix docs; add explicit 1-voxel halo sampling if seams matter.

10. **Low** probe_grid.rs:97-160: when target dims > source dims (or non-divisible), adjacent probe regions overlap (`.max(z_start+1)`) → duplicate sampling; harmless but wasteful; also no early-out. Fix: fine.

11. **Medium** reality.rs:369-412: every gate crossing clones stamps + drifts + consumed vectors, re-inserts into BTreeMaps (O(S log S)), and re-sorts consumed_supplies (O(C log C)) even though input is already sorted. Gate crossings are rare but the snapshot is also serialized per chunk request (to_words) — clone-heavy design; with many stamps/drifts (long play sessions) each crossing becomes O(S log S + C log C) with multiple full copies (3-4 Vec clones). Fix: persistent/COW data structure or in-place mutation with versioned snapshots; at minimum, sort only when constructing from untrusted input, and reuse sorted order.

12. **Low** reality.rs:423-426: fingerprint() materializes a full Vec<u32> copy of the snapshot (O(S+D+C) allocation) per call — stream the hash directly (hash_words could take an iterator) to avoid allocation; same for to_words (needed for transport, fine).

13. **Low** reality.rs:236-286/300-367: stamps/drifts/consumed_supplies grow monotonically without eviction (unbounded in infinite-world play; transport and fingerprint cost grow with playtime). Fix: eviction policy for drifts (e.g., only track cells within N cells of current position, or age out), acknowledging identity-semantics tradeoff.

14. **Low** generate_maze.rs:55: per-iteration `Vec::new()` for up-to-4 neighbors — N small heap allocations per maze; use a fixed-size array [Option;4] or stack tuple. Also could skip the frontier re-pick cost. Minor.

15. **Low** architecture.rs:596-598 (`host()` linear scan) used in loops at 663/692 → O(O·H); and validate_architecture O(H² + O² + L·O) — validation-only, fine, but host() could be a HashMap<HostId, usize> index if assemblies grow.

16. **Medium** bsp.rs:126-148 contains_point: epsilon band recursion explores BOTH children when |distance| ≤ eps; a point lying on k coincident cut planes costs O(2^k); degenerate deep BSP (recursion depth D up to P) risks stack overflow for large polygon soups; also `Solid::contains_point` rebuilds BSP per call (documented, but if a voxelizer calls contains_point per voxel per solid, that's O(V·P log P) — recommend caching bsp()). Also `clip_polygons` recursion depth = BSP depth; balanced build O(log P), but build() uses first polygon's plane without pivot selection → pathological input (e.g., sorted parallel planes) yields O(P) depth and O(P²) build. Fix: median/pivot plane selection; iterative traversal or explicit stack for contains/clip; memoize bsp().

Wait — is build really O(P²) worst case? Each build level splits polygons; with no pivot selection, depth can be P and each polygon can be split once per level → total splits O(P·D) = O(P²) worst; typical O(P log P). And recursion depth = D = O(P) worst → stack overflow risk for big soups. Realistic CSG usage: door cuts with ~24 polygons — small. Severity Low-Medium.

17. **Low** solid.rs:84-87: `Solid::contains_point` builds a fresh BSP per call — documented, but callers doing voxelization must build once; the API invites accidental O(V·P log P). Suggest caching or a `&BspNode` variant.

18. **Low** bsp.rs:27-56 build: `self.plane.get_or_insert_with(|| polygons[0].plane)` — no polygon-area/pivot heuristic. Covered in 16.

19. **Low** level_builder.rs:58-62: corner columns written twice (north+east walls both true → h-loop runs twice on same column); and floor+wall+ceiling per column are separate set() calls with bounds-checked index (3-4 index computations per column); trivial. Could hoist a raw-slice write. Also wall columns between adjacent rooms are 2 voxels thick (each room authors its own wall) — intentional? It doubles wall voxel count vs shared walls; mention as note.

20. **Low** voxel_grid.rs: light duplicated as scalar + rgb (memory 6B/voxel, e.g. 512×64×512 = 16.8M voxels → ~100MB per grid; 4 arrays) — could compute scalar on the fly when needed (max of 3 bytes) saving 1 byte/voxel; minor. Also no methods to bulk-write; fine.

21. **Low** probe_grid.rs index: `(z * height * width + y * width + x) * 3` matches the z-major iteration in from_voxel_grid (z outer) — cache-friendly. Good.

22. Concurrency notes (domain): zero shared mutable state; thread-compatible. Deterministic RNG. One nuance: `generate_maze` uses the caller's RNG — if two chunks generate concurrently from the same RNG instance (not possible with &mut), fine. And `bake_path_traced_lighting` re-seeds 42 per call → deterministic but spatially correlated noise (same directions for every voxel across chunks) — could cause banding; seed per chunk.

Also check: does the maze generation guarantee connectivity through walls? Standard. The loop-breaking phase may open walls on the maze BORDER (nx,ny out of bounds → wall opened but no neighbor) — border cells get a wall knocked out pointing outside the map; if the voxelizer uses grid border walls as the world edge, this creates openings at the world border (leak). Note as Low: loop-breaking opens walls without checking border (line 113-127: `cell.walls[dir] = false` even when the neighbor is out of bounds). Actually is that a bug? In the Backrooms context the border may be handled elsewhere (chunk borders...). It opens a hole in the outer shell of the generated maze. Only affects the loop phase at border cells — random cells — probability small. Worth a Low finding: guard border cells.

Let me verify that border behavior: loop at line ~104-127: pick random (x,y), check zone, `dir = rng 0..4`, `cell.walls[dir] = false` unconditionally, then if neighbor in bounds → open opposite. So yes: border cells can lose an outward wall. Low.

Also line 99: `if cell.zone == Blackout { continue; }` — good.

Maze determinism: the generator is pure w.r.t. rng — good.

Also `Level0Builder` — walls: `voxels.set(vx, h, vz, wall_type)` inside h loop 1..=wh — for wh=3 (small) fine.

One more possible perf item: `Grid` cells are `Cell` (Copy? No — Cell has Clone+PartialEq+Eq; visited: bool, walls: [bool;4], zone: Copy enum, light_level u8, is_corridor bool — it's not Copy because not derived; it is Clone. Fine.)

Let me also double-check `get_recursive` early returns vs mask: line 91-93: if mask bit clear → Some((0,0,[0;3],0)) — good; note it does NOT descend into a child (child may not exist if built by a builder that only allocates used children? All builders allocate 8 slots at split, so children always exist. build_octree also pushes 8. OK).

But WAIT — one more real bug possibility in get_recursive: child slot index for masked-off octant returns early — good. For set_recursive internal: `child_node_idx = child_base_index + octant_idx` — if mask bit was clear and the tree was built by a DIFFERENT builder that only allocated set children... all builders allocate 8. OK.

Now also check SvoNode is 20 bytes? Leaf: u8 + u32 + [u8;3] + u8 = 4+4+4+4 = 16 (with padding); Internal: u32+u8 = 8+pad. enum → 24 bytes (tag+payload). Fine.

Now concurrency inventory table for the domain:

- No statics, no atomics, no threads, no unsafe in src/domain (grep verified).
- Shared mutable state: none. All types Send+Sync by construction (plain data, no interior mutability).
- RNG: StdRng local to bake (path_tracer.rs:108); MazeGenerator::generate(&mut R) caller-provided (generate_maze.rs:23) — no sharing across threads possible via &mut.
- Determinism: mix64/StdRng-seeded — deterministic; pillar_shape/pit hazards/pit sort — deterministic.
- Cross-boundary: RealitySnapshot is the "request identity" passed by value to workers (per module docs) — it's Clone, immutable after construction; safe to send across the wasm worker boundary via structured clone of u32 words; no locks needed. Note: from_words/to_words provide the serialization; no data races by construction. The one re-entrancy consideration: workers receive snapshot by value — fine.

So concurrency section: mostly "no issues found; the design is sound; caveats: (1) if bake_path_traced_lighting is ever run in workers with the same seed 42, all chunks get identical light patterns — seed per chunk; (2) growing snapshot serialization cost per chunk request (transport), not a race.

Ranked high-leverage optimizations:
1. Path tracer: replace per-solid-voxel 64-ray hemisphere casts with per-air-voxel irradiance + scatter, or light-source DDA propagation: cuts ~6-30× and removes transcendental cost. (path_tracer.rs)
2. SVO: fix mask-clear + add arena freelist/compaction: bounds memory for editable octrees, restores is_empty semantics; plus get/set are O(depth) — fine.
3. RealitySnapshot: incremental/COW updates and streaming fingerprint: removes O(S log S + C log C) per gate crossing and per-request allocation.
4. BSP: pivot-plane selection + cached BSP for contains_point; bounds worst-case O(P²).
5. Maze: fixed-size neighbor buffer (micro).
6. Level builder: shared walls between adjacent cells + hoisted index writes (micro; only if wall voxels are doubled intentionally, skip).

Also worth one more check — `SurfaceQualityProfile::sanitized` clones — trivial.

Also `player_vitals` — O(1) — fine. `supplies` — O(1). `fixture` — O(1). `environment` — O(1). `world_topology` — O(1) address math.

Let me now also quickly verify there are no `#[allow(dead_code)]`-style hot loops I missed, e.g., in `mod.rs` of entities. Also double-check `Cell` derives lack Hash but zone is used in maze compare — fine.

One more potential O(n²): `ProbeGrid::from_voxel_grid` — `grid.get` per voxel is fine.

`RealitySnapshot::with_supply_consumed` — binary search + insert O(C) per consume (Vec insert shift) — O(C) fine.

   89|         let mut actual_openings = 0;
   90|         let mut attempts = 0;
   91| 
   92|         while actual_openings < extra_openings && attempts < extra_openings * 3 {
   93|             attempts += 1;
   94|             let x = rng.random_range(0..width);
   95|             let y = rng.random_range(0..depth);
   96| 
   97|             // Blackout zones are knot-like dead-ends; suppress extra loops in them
   98|             if let Some(cell) = grid.get(x, y) {
   99|                 if cell.zone == crate::domain::entities::cell::MicrobiomeZone::Blackout {
  100|                     continue;
  101|                 }
  102|             }
  103| 
  104|             let dir = rng.random_range(0..4);
  105| 
  106|             if let Some(cell) = grid.get_mut(x, y) {
  107|                 cell.walls[dir] = false;
  108|             }
  109| 
  110|             let (nx, ny) = match dir {
  111|                 0 => (x as isize, y as isize - 1),
  112|                 1 => (x as isize + 1, y as isize),
  113|                 2 => (x as isize, y as isize + 1),
  114|                 3 => (x as isize - 1, y as isize),
  115|                 _ => unreachable!(),
  116|             };
  117| 
  118|             if nx >= 0 && nx < width as isize && ny >= 0 && ny < depth as isize {
  119|                 let (nx, ny) = (nx as usize, ny as usize);
  120|                 let opposite_dir = (dir + 2) % 4;
  121|                 if let Some(n_cell) = grid.get_mut(nx, ny) {
  122|                     n_cell.walls[opposite_dir] = false;
  123|                 }
  124|             }
  125| 
  126|             actual_openings += 1;
  127|         }
  128|     }
  129| }

   27|     /// Inserts polygons, extending the tree wherever they cross regions.
   28|     pub fn build(&mut self, polygons: Vec<Polygon>) {
   29|         if polygons.is_empty() {
   30|             return;
   31|         }
   32|         let plane = *self.plane.get_or_insert_with(|| polygons[0].plane);
   33| 
   34|         let mut front = Vec::new();
   35|         let mut back = Vec::new();
   36|         // Coplanar fragments live on this node, facing either way.
   37|         let mut coplanar_front = Vec::new();
   38|         let mut coplanar_back = Vec::new();
   39|         for polygon in &polygons {
   40|             plane.split_polygon(
   41|                 polygon,
   42|                 &mut coplanar_front,
   43|                 &mut coplanar_back,
   44|                 &mut front,
   45|                 &mut back,
   46|             );
   47|         }
   48|         self.polygons.extend(coplanar_front);
   49|         self.polygons.extend(coplanar_back);
   50|         if !front.is_empty() {
   51|             self.front.get_or_insert_with(Box::default).build(front);
   52|         }
   53|         if !back.is_empty() {
   54|             self.back.get_or_insert_with(Box::default).build(back);
   55|         }
   56|     }
   57| 
   58|     /// Converts solid space to empty space and back.
   59|     pub fn invert(&mut self) {
   60|         for polygon in &mut self.polygons {
   61|             *polygon = polygon.flipped();
   62|         }
   63|         if let Some(plane) = &mut self.plane {
   64|             *plane = plane.flipped();
   65|         }
   66|         if let Some(front) = &mut self.front {
   67|             front.invert();
   68|         }
   69|         if let Some(back) = &mut self.back {
   70|             back.invert();
   71|         }
   72|         std::mem::swap(&mut self.front, &mut self.back);
   73|     }
   74| 
   75|     /// Returns the fragments of `polygons` lying in empty space of this
   76|     /// tree; fragments inside the solid are removed.
   77|     pub fn clip_polygons(&self, polygons: Vec<Polygon>) -> Vec<Polygon> {
   78|         let Some(plane) = self.plane else {
   79|             return polygons;
   80|         };
   81|         let mut front = Vec::new();
   82|         let mut back = Vec::new();
   83|         let mut coplanar_front = Vec::new();
   84|         let mut coplanar_back = Vec::new();
   85|         for polygon in &polygons {
   86|             plane.split_polygon(
   87|                 polygon,
   88|                 &mut coplanar_front,
   89|                 &mut coplanar_back,
   90|                 &mut front,
   91|                 &mut back,
   92|             );
   93|         }
   94|         // Coplanar fragments clip with the region they face into.
   95|         front.extend(coplanar_front);
   96|         back.extend(coplanar_back);
   97|         let mut result = match &self.front {
   98|             Some(node) => node.clip_polygons(front),
   99|             None => front, // no front child: region is outside, keep
  100|         };
  101|         if let Some(node) = &self.back {
  102|             result.extend(node.clip_polygons(back));
  103|         } // no back child: region is inside the solid, discard
  104|         result
  105|     }
  106| 
  107|     /// Removes every part of this tree's polygons inside `other`'s solid.
  108|     pub fn clip_to(&mut self, other: &BspNode) {
  109|         self.polygons = other.clip_polygons(std::mem::take(&mut self.polygons));
  110|         if let Some(front) = &mut self.front {
  111|             front.clip_to(other);
  112|         }
  113|         if let Some(back) = &mut self.back {
  114|             back.clip_to(other);
  115|         }
  116|     }
  117| 
  118|     /// Every polygon in the tree.
  119|     pub fn all_polygons(&self) -> Vec<Polygon> {
  120|         let mut polygons = self.polygons.clone();
  121|         if let Some(front) = &self.front {
  122|             polygons.extend(front.all_polygons());
  123|         }
  124|         if let Some(back) = &self.back {
  125|             polygons.extend(back.all_polygons());
  126|         }
  127|         polygons
  128|     }

  130|     /// Is `point` inside the solid this tree bounds? Points within epsilon
  131|     /// of a boundary count as inside, so voxelization fills surfaces.

  145|                 Some(node) => node.contains_point(point),
  146|                 None => true,
  147|             }
  148|         } else {

  150|             let front_solid = self
